## Claude

I've claimed `GSS_POWER_THEFT_FIX_WRONG_VICTIM` and started a background agent on the fix. It hasn't reported yet, so nothing is built or published.

- **The defect:** `CompPowerTap` adds up stolen energy across victims, then raises the theft event against whichever faction is the victim at that moment. If the clamp switches from faction A to faction B before the event fires, B is charged for A's draining.
- **The fix:** when the victim changes, charge the accumulated energy to the previous victim, so each faction pays only for its own.
- **The test:** the agent will add an offline selftest (the item's L0 bar) that drains A, switches to B, and checks each is charged only its own energy.
- **Scope:** it builds and runs the selftests, then commits and publishes. It does not deploy.
- **Still owed afterwards:** the item's live proof row (L2), which needs the bridge. The bridge's test-profile grant is still expired.

I'm holding the lease until the agent reports. Then I'll record the result against the criteria, as I did for the restring fix.

## Claude

I've claimed `AUDITOR_PRESCREEN_FIX_WINDOW_SINCE_LAST_RUN` and started a background agent on it. Nothing is built or published yet.

- **The defect:** the Auditor's pre-launch screen looks only at the last day (`audit_prescreen.py --days 1`). A refused or failed daily run therefore leaves that day's closes never screened. A 7-day window was already tried and re-raised about 57 hard flags per run.
- **The fix:** a run that finishes successfully writes a stamp, and the next screen starts from that stamp. With no stamp it falls back to one day and says so. An explicit `--days` still overrides, and the window is capped at a sane maximum.
- **The tests:** the agent writes offline selftests for three cases. A skipped day gets screened on the next run, a missing stamp falls back to one day, and a failed run doesn't advance the stamp.
- **Safety:** it won't launch the real Auditor or touch its live state.

I'm holding the lease until the agent reports. Then I'll record the result against the item's verify line.

<result>**Verdict: HARNESS/SITE, not MOD. The Droidworks deploy is not the cause.**

- **Failed row:** `&lt;canary red: HARNESS&gt;`, with the reason "no live colonist". It is the same in both jobs, from `testq/done/&lt;job&gt;.json` `result.rows[0]`. The canary is `modcheck/validity.py canary()`. It fails before any chain runs, so no droid_dig row ever ran.
- **Not Droidworks:** the same "no live colonist" canary red appears on jobs with an empty `deploy`: ac97fa, 4421e9, b87a55, 35527f, 162505, 107a99 and bbef21. J20261029T155741Z-323412 (a guard job with no deploy) went red three minutes after 75e3dd, probably on the same game.
- **Why 75e3dd looks different:** the deploy only forced a relaunch (load 85 s against 1 s). The guard jobs that passed (for example 0ea2da at 15:42) ran with load 1 s and no deploy.
- **What it points to:** the colony is dead or missing on the Playing map by canary time. In the droid/trench red jobs the run phase is about 350-390 s before the red, but I could not read the modcheck logs (the runner host isn't mounted here) to confirm what happened in that time. A near-identical case is logged in `tick.log` at 2026-10-29T03:28: "dead colony on a Playing map … game stopped (rw_game.sh down) so the next job cold-starts a fresh colony".
- **Current state:** the stevedesktop lane is PAUSED by 323412 (`paused.json`). Any resubmit will sit until the lane is resumed.
- **Fix:** have the runner owner stop the SteveDesktop game (`rw_game.sh down`) and run `testq.py resume --reason "dead colony, game restarted"`. Then resubmit droid_dig with `--deploy Droidworks` and the canary left on. The canary is relevant here, because "no live colonist" invalidates a droid dig test. `--no-canary` would not fix it and is not acceptable.
- **Owed:** someone should find out why the colony dies during the roughly 6-minute run phase before the canary. That needs `/mnt/d/RimTest/testq/attempts/&lt;job&gt;.a1/modcheck.log` on the runner host, which I couldn't reach.

Notes are in `D:\ClaudeWork\rimmandrake\foundry_dd\canary.md`. I changed nothing else.</result>

## Claude

I claimed `RAISE_ORDER_FIX_FILING_DATE_OUTRANKS_PLAN` and started it. An opus `rm-builder` agent is fixing it in the background, and I have no result yet.

The bug is that `rimflow next` offers raised items in the order they were filed, not the order they were raised. That was why a 10-23 ticket beat a goal-1 ticket raised in the same Producer run. The agent will:
- make raised items come out in raise order, for both ready and proposed items;
- keep unraised items from ever beating raised ones;
- add a selftest that files A, B, C, raises C, A, B, and checks that `next` offers C, A, B;
- run the existing raise and priority selftests, then commit and publish.

I'll record the commit on the item when it reports back.

## Claude

`RAISE_ORDER_FIX_FILING_DATE_OUTRANKS_PLAN` is fixed and done: `rimflow next` now offers raised items in the order they were raised, not the order they were filed. The code is published as `c9f67e7cf`, and the ledger close as `c9af26496`.

- **Fix:** each item stores the time of its first raise, and raised items sort by it, ahead of every unraised item. This applies to both ready and proposed items. When both the top ready item and the top proposed item are raised, `next` offers whichever was raised first. Before, the ready one always won. The changes are in `src/RimMandrake/rimflow/model.py`, `priority.py` and `cli.py`.
- **Test:** the new `selftest_raise_order.py` files A, B, C, raises C, A, B, and checks that `next` offers C, A, B, then an older unraised item. It passes 10/10 and fails 7 of 10 on the old code. Three other selftests (`selftest_block_frontier`, `selftest_lease`, `selftest_built_old_reader`) are red because this clone lacks the old commits they compare against, not because of this change. The agent filed a lesson on it.
- **Publish:** the first `./publish` was refused because someone else's uncommitted `OWNER.jsonl` edit was in the way. The agent's stash was blocked by a hook, so I stashed it myself, published, and restored it.
- **Still owed:** a code review, since the four source files still show DIRTY.

## Claude

I claimed `ARENA_TASKS_BUILD_FROZEN_BEFORE_ASSIGNED` (item 1 of 16 in the architecture-trial build order). It is Opus-class work, so an Opus `rm-builder` agent is building it and I have no result yet.

The agent builds `arena_tasks.py census` and `freeze`. The census scans closed items from 10-12 to 10-27 and labels each Clean or Escaped. It writes one task JSON per item, keeping the hidden checks only in the judge's directory. It also freezes each task file's hash. The selftest must prove each requirement by forced failure:
- a changed task file is refused by its hash;
- a task with `live: full` is caught;
- a leaked hidden check is caught;
- a missing sanity probe item is caught.

The agent runs the real census once. It will report the pool count and the sanity probe result, and treat a round or zero count as a query bug until the probe shows the instrument can see.

The agent cannot declare the item done. The item's own rules require a different agent to test it, then a blind GPT review before close. I'll set up both once the build lands.

Ranked findings, all from `src/RimMandrake/Utils/arena/`:
1. **P1, `selftest_arena_tasks.py:295-297`.** The test passes because the fixture clone sits at final HEAD and already holds the hidden tests. I read the line: the test only proves the `git ls-files` exemption and says nothing about real isolation. Real, and it undercuts the "hidden checks absent" criterion.
2. **P1, `arena_tasks.py:559-566`.** Any file in `git ls-files` is exempt from the scan. A tracked file can hold a hidden body, and the exemption never checks that its content matches base. Real.
3. **P1, `arena_tasks.py:615-616`.** Files over 4 MiB are skipped silently, so padding hides a leak. Real but cheap to fix: flag oversize files instead of skipping them.
4. **P1, `arena_tasks.py:668-685`.** `load_frozen` never calls `find_leaks`. It also hashes the file at 677 and re-reads it at 683, which leaves a race. Real. Fix by reading the bytes once, hashing them, then parsing those same bytes.
5. **P1, `arena_tasks.py:481-498`.** `read_log` globs every `*.jsonl` in sorted order, and `frozen_hashes` trusts the first match. A file named `000.jsonl` holding a forged freeze record would win. Real if the events directory is writable. The impact depends on who can write there.
6. **P1, `arena_tasks.py:632`.** The assign check and the freeze append are not under one lock, so a time-of-check/time-of-use gap exists. Real but narrow.
7. **P1, `arena_tasks.py:347-353, 393-394`.** A Clean label can be frozen before its 7-day window closes. A later rework would make it wrong. Real. Fix by refusing to freeze Clean until the window has ended.
8. **P1, `arena_tasks.py:356-365`.** The prompt is read from the closing commit's parent, not from claim time. This conflicts with the spec ("prompt as at claim time"). Real, and it needs an owner or design call.
9. **P1/P2, `arena_tasks.py:547-555, 617-623`.** The scan matches raw multiline tokens only, so a JSON-escaped body evades it. Bodies under 40 characters get no token at all. Real, and this is leak-detection blindness.
10. **P2, `arena_tasks.py:318-322`.** An item reopened after its close, or whose last close falls outside the window, is dropped without a reason, and the scanned count does not include it. Census blindness. Real, though possibly intended.
11. **P2, `arena_tasks.py:238-245`.** A deleted test counts as objective proof. Real.
12. **P2, `arena_tasks.py:152-175, 468-474`.** Three smaller issues:
    - Commit resolving matches on author, date and subject alone.
    - `write_pool` keeps stale frozen files, so the reported pool and the stored tasks can diverge.
    - Freeze accepts a missing task file.

GPT's other P1 points (symlink aliases, tracked-file exemption beyond the above) are in the saved file.

My overall read: GPT would not sign off on the "hidden checks absent" criterion. The hash-refusal and `live: full` criteria hold for ordinary edits.</result>

<result>Criteria 2 and 4 pass; criteria 1 and 3 fail. I changed no code, made no commit, didn't touch the ledger and never used the real arena root.

- **C1 agent-written event rejected: FAIL.** The signing itself holds: an unsigned line, a wrong token, a replayed line, an edited or deleted middle line, reordered lines, a duplicate line and a line copied in from another arm are all rejected. A token file inside the tasks or clones folders, or linked into one, is refused. What breaks is the threat model:
  - **D1 (high): any arm agent can read the token and write accepted events.** Agents run as the same user, and on the `/mnt/d` drive a file created with mode 0600 shows as 777. The arm-readable list in `arena_tasks` is not enforced. `HarnessWriter()` reads the token for any process that imports it, so a write from an agent process was accepted. This confirms the risk the builder flagged as unverified.
  - **D2 (high): deleting the last line goes unnoticed.** An agent deleted its own self-test `test_start`, and the next harness write left 0 rejected and 0 flagged. Deleting the whole file reads as an empty log. Nothing outside the file records how long it should be.
  - **D3 (medium):** a final line cut off with no newline swallows the harness's next record, which is then lost.
  - Smaller issues: owner minutes accepts True, -30, NaN and inf (D6). Deleting the first line rejects every later valid line; that is detected, but the data is lost (D7).
- **C2 adapter vs `rimflow_metrics.py` close counts: PASS.** On 10-25, 10-26 and 10-28, my recount, metrics' `flow.done` and the adapter agree (92, 461, 80) with identical item sets; all time is 3527 each. "Done transitions" matches `flow.done`: closes, verifies and implemented events all count, and nothing else moves an item to done.
  - **D5 (medium):** running the adapter's `--apply` twice doubles the counts (10-25 goes from 92 done to 184).
  - For v3, builder and tester ids are seat names like FOUNDRY, and a ledger verify becomes `test_end`, not `test_start`. So the different-agent rule can only be measured for v3 through testq.
- **C3 tester equal to builder flagged: FAIL (D4, medium).** Exact matches are flagged; a testq tester and a genuinely different agent are not; a missing or empty builder id is refused. But "AGENTX" against "agentX", or " agentX " against "agentX", is not flagged and counts as a valid different-agent test. A builder id of whitespace or the number 7 is also accepted. A builder who runs ahead of testing is not reported; only latency is.
- **C4 owner minutes per arm: PASS.** My fixture's hand count (10.5 minutes, kept per arm, task T1 counted as owner-unblocked) matches.
  - **D8 (low):** a task judged accept and then reject still counts as accepted (4 and 3 points against my 3 and 2). A card sent twice is counted twice.

Defects by rank: D1 &gt; D2 &gt; D4 &gt; D5 &gt; D3 &gt; D6 &gt; D8 &gt; D7. None of them is filed in rimflow or lessons yet, because the brief said not to touch the ledger, so filing them is still owed.

## Claude

The independent test of the step 2 event log failed two of its four criteria, so `ARENA_EVENTLOG_BUILD_AGENTS_MAY_NOT_WRITE` cannot close. I've started a fix round on an Opus builder, which makes four agents running again: fix rounds for steps 1 and 2, the gate builder and the judge builder. The independent test of the metering is still queued.

- **Passed:**
  - **Adapter counts:** the tester recounted three days of ledger activity (10-25, 10-26, 10-28) itself, and its figures, `rimflow_metrics.py` and the adapter agree (92, 461, 80).
  - **Owner minutes:** the tester's hand-computed fixture gave 10.5 minutes, matching the tool.
  - **Signing:** unsigned lines, wrong tokens, replays, edits, deletions and reordering are all rejected.
- **Failed:**
  - **Token is readable (high).** Any arm agent runs as the same user, so it can read the token and write accepted events; the tester confirmed an agent-process write was accepted. On the `/mnt/d` drive, a file created with mode 0600 shows as 777. This was the risk the builder flagged as unverified.
  - **Deleting the last line, or the whole file, goes unnoticed (high).** Nothing outside the file records how long it should be.
  - **Case and whitespace in ids (medium).** Same-agent detection is exact-case, so "AGENTX" against "agentX" counts as a valid different-agent test.
  - **Adapter is not idempotent (medium).** Running `--apply` twice doubles the counts.
  - **Truncated last line (medium).** A line cut off with no newline swallows the next record.
  - **Smaller:** owner minutes accepts True, -30, NaN and inf. A task judged accept then reject still counts as accepted. A card sent twice counts twice.
- **Fix round:** it does the best available on the token: keep it outside arm-readable roots and refuse to write when the arm marker is set. It also makes tampering detectable by anchoring the chain's head hash and length where arms can't reach, so a forged or deleted event is caught at report time. The agent must state the residual risk honestly, because a same-user token can't be truly hidden. If the anchor needs a rimflow event, it will describe it and I'll decide whether to run it.

The tester filed nothing, so I'll file the defects when the fixes land.

  - An out-of-area edit fails.
  - A failed canary, a missing modcheck and a canary for another sha each give `invalid`.
  - A builder id equal to the tester id fails, and so does a missing or hand-appended unsigned `test_start`.
  - If every step is unrunnable, the gate does not pass.
- **Not self-reportable:** the verdict is a signed gate event written through the event log writer, plus a JSON record. The gate refuses to run from inside an arm clone.
- **Offline limits:** the steps ran against stub tools in a throwaway repo. The real `winbuild`, `validate_patch` and deploy plan have never run inside a gate.
- **Design choice for you to be aware of:** the design names only pass, fail and invalid. A step that can't run gives UNMEASURED, and the overall verdict becomes `invalid`, so the arm isn't blamed and the run can be re-queued. A diff that edits a gate instrument such as `run_selftests.py` also reads UNMEASURED for that step.
- **Dependency:** the live canary check expects a signed `canary` event plus a `live_release` verdict. The step 8+ lane wrapper must emit exactly that.

The tester probes unrunnable steps, sneaky placeholder diffs, scope tricks, forged test records and canaries, hand-written gate results, and a dirty working tree leaking into the verdict.

Four agents are running now: the fix rounds for steps 1 and 2, the judge builder (step 4), and this gate tester. The metering tester is still queued.

**Fixed, each with a forced-failure test:**

- **Symlinks:** ones into the judge directory or outside the roots are flagged, not followed.

## Claude

Fix round 2 for the step 2 event log (`ARENA_EVENTLOG_BUILD_AGENTS_MAY_NOT_WRITE`) is done and published as `8e2efb53c6`. It still needs a second independent test and a final GPT pass before it can close.

**Review result:** all 9 GPT findings were real, and all 9 are fixed. The selftest went from 81 to 131 checks, and the new tests fail 27 times against the old code.

**What changed:**
- **Writes:** each harness pass opens a capability file, and only the four harness scripts can sign. Every record carries its process chain and an environment fingerprint.
- **Missing anchors:** a non-empty log with no usable anchor is refused as UNMEASURED. A report is "trusted" only when a ledger-published anchor covers the whole log.
- **Adapter scoring:** ledger closes with no judge events report "not comparable" instead of 0 points.
- **Counting:** tests are matched per (task, build id). The whole log is sorted by event time, so a test before its build is flagged. Malformed cards are skipped and counted.
- **Testers:** the tester for a testq job is the seat that submitted it (`requested_by`), with the runner host recorded. V3 can tell seats apart but not individual agents.
- **Sampled day:** 2026-10-24 gives 138 vs 138 done and 113 vs 113 close.
- **Anchors and selftest:** a bad anchor is listed in `anchor_errors` instead of crashing. The selftest no longer touches an inherited `ARENA_ANCHOR_FILE`; before the fix it overwrote a decoy anchor store.

**Residual risk:** any process running as the same user that can read the token can still call `_sign` directly. The capability file and process stamps only make forgery visible afterwards. Only running the harness as a separate OS user closes it, and that needs your decision.

**Gate dependency:** the writer now refuses any write made without an open pass. The production `arena_gate.py` `finish()` must open one. I relayed that to the gate fix round. The code is still DIRTY.

I started a second independent Opus tester for the event log. Its probes cover bypassing the capability pass, the anchor and "trusted" logic, adapter idempotence, interleaved builds, clock skew, and a fresh sampled day. Four agents are running: fix rounds for steps 3 and 4, plus the retests of steps 1 and 2. The metering tester is still queued.

## Claude

- `Agent — Fix round 3 for arena event log`

## Claude

The second independent test of the step 2 event log (`ARENA_EVENTLOG_BUILD_AGENTS_MAY_NOT_WRITE`, after fix round 2) says it still doesn't pass. Two of the four criteria pass and two are partial. I started fix round 3 on an Opus builder, and nothing in this report is filed in the ledger.

**Passed:**
- **Adapter counts (criterion 2):** on 2026-10-23 the adapter and `rimflow_metrics.py` agree at 111, and the 54 closes match the tester's raw-ledger count item for item. On 2026-10-21 both give 52. Applying twice or in shuffled order writes nothing new.
- **Owner minutes (criterion 4):** per arm, with and without owner-unblocked work.

**High defects:**
- **Trust without a token.** An attacker needs no token to get `trusted=True` from `report()`:
  - delete the records after the newest ledger anchor and trim the local anchors;
  - post a forged `ARENA_ANCHOR` note from any seat on any item;
  - delete an arm that was never published, log and anchors, so it drops out of the report and overall trust flips from False to True.

  Setting `__main__.__file__` in one line also gets past the entrypoint check.
- **The testq side reads nothing.** `load_testq` finds 0 records in the real testq folder, whose 447 done records are wrapped as {job, result, attempts}.

**Medium:**
- **Re-runs:** a testq re-run with the same job id is silently dropped, so a PASS after a FAIL is lost.
- **Self-test flags:** a self-test is not flagged if it names a non-matching build id, or a sha when the build has a build id.
- **Id lookalikes:** zero-width or fullwidth versions of an id count as a different agent.
- **Submitter `BENCH.filler`:** it counts as a different agent on a BENCH build, and it submitted 367 of the 447 jobs.
- **Ordering:** clock skew or equal timestamps aren't flagged.

**Low:** card dedupe ignores case and spaces. Minutes of 10**309 crash. Anchor counts like `5.0` are dropped without being listed as errors.

Fix round 3:
- It requires ledger anchor notes to carry a signature from the harness token.
- It keeps a signed, append-only roster of arms.
- It parses the real testq shape, using a copy of 5 real done records as the test fixture.
- It normalises ids and maps `BENCH.filler` to its seat.
- It fixes the low-severity items.
- It must again state the same-user residual risk plainly.

Four agents are running: fix rounds for steps 3, 4 and 2, plus the step 1 retest. The metering tester is still queued.

## Claude

The second independent test of the step 1 tasks tool (`ARENA_TASKS_BUILD_FROZEN_BEFORE_ASSIGNED`) says it still fails its fourth criterion: hidden checks leak into arm-readable paths by 15 routes the scan doesn't see. Three criteria pass: the census counts reproduce, every changed task file is refused by its hash, and all 4 tasks have `live: none`. I started fix round 2. Nothing from this report is filed in the ledger.

**Leak routes still open:**
- a bare repo anywhere in the clone root;
- an arm-written `.git/arena_clone.json` with a fake or null task, base or source;
- commit and tag messages;
- a `tasks/*.json` inside a clone;
- a text file named like a pack file, or a fake loose object;
- compressed or encoded copies, a body split across two files, and filenames.

**Other defects:**
- **False positive:** an arm's notes naming its own test path flag a leak. The scan is global, so that blocks freezing and loading for every task.
- **Freeze:** it accepts a task file that is a symlink, and doesn't see assign records under other names. A file swapped mid-freeze is refused at load, but then the frozen task can never be replaced.
- **Census labels:** `ART_EVAL_SHADOW_FIX_PLANT_KEPT_ROWS` is wrongly labelled Escaped. Its "rework" says "Remaining half of" the parent and touches none of its files, so the labelled pool is 3, not 4. The census also drops children filed seconds before a close and never follows a review's findings.
- **Claim-time prompt:** the base-parent prompt differs from the claim-time prose in 1 of 4 tasks (`STEVEDESKTOP`). There the prose was committed 2 minutes after start, so the base gives the better prompt.

**Pool ruling.** The census has not applied the Conductor's pool ruling of 2026-10-30 01:59Z, recorded in the item's history:
- Clean/Escaped labels become optional metadata, so unlabelled items enter the pool.
- The start-event requirement is dropped.
- The base is the closing sha's parent.
- The >8-file cap stays.
- The pre-10-28 shas get pinned against gc under `refs/arena/`.

Fix round 2 applies that ruling and re-runs the real census without freezing. It also replaces the blacklist leak scan with a whitelist proof. The harness builds each arm clone as a fresh shallow clone at `base_sha`. `check` then recomputes the exact file set and the expected objects, and keeps the clone marker in a harness-owned location. The old substring scan stays as a secondary check.

Four agents are running: fix rounds for steps 1, 2, 3 and 4. The metering tester is still queued.

Fix round 1 for the step 3 gate (`ARENA_GATE_BUILD_UNMEASURED_NEVER_PASSES`) is done and published as `3b420bee1476`, and a blind GPT review of it is now running on a Sonnet agent. The gate selftest passes 95/95. The old gate fails 39 of the new checks, and the variant that counts UNMEASURED as a pass still fails 24, so the mutation check still catches it. The code is DIRTY.

**Fixed, each with a test:**
- **Signing key.** Gate steps run in a bwrap sandbox with an allow-listed environment, the clean checkout as the working directory, an empty HOME, and the token directories masked. Signing happens only in the parent process after the steps end. The tester's probe for reading the token now verifies 0 forged records.
- **Hidden failures.** Tests run through the gate's own isolated runner, and a pass needs a nonce-bound record of check counts from that runner. Diffs touching harness-imported files, `rimflow/` or stdlib-shadow names are refused.

T:251-254 uses a fake Linux executable, so the "cannot open the arm map" criterion is proven only for the Linux sandbox. This is a design call: either accept and document it, or tighten it.
2. **`SystemExit(0)` during a hidden-check import counts as a pass** (J:603-606). Real. The runner treats exit code 0 or None as success and sets `ok=True`. T:407-410 tests `os._exit` but not `sys.exit(0)`. A known-bad replay could slip through.
3. **A repo-configured `textconv` can run a command during diff generation** (J:533-534). Real. `--no-ext-diff` does not disable textconv, and `_git` at J:508-511 passes no `-c` overrides.
4. **Judges can read the raw, un-neutralised head tree** (J:555-569, 760, 816). Plausible and consistent with finding 1 (the sandbox is not enforced for GPT). A design call that depends on where `tree` sits relative to the judge's reachable paths.
5. **Judges are not isolated from each other** (J:285-291, 759-800). Plausible. They share one `work` directory and the inherited environment. Real, and probably worth fixing with a per-judge workspace.
6. **Neutraliser blind spots** (J:329-380, 486-488). `xralph_afoo`-style glued names and `import trio` or `v3.2` survive. Partly a design call, since the docstring says imports are kept deliberately; the glued-name case is a real gap.
7. **`--name-only` without `-z` quotes odd filenames and can silently drop their changes** (J:530-534). Real. The names are reused as pathspecs, and git quotes tabs, newlines and non-ASCII characters.
8. **Missing hidden-check files fail open** (J:915-918). Real. When no checks resolve, the code skips them and judges normally, so the replay criterion holds only when the check file exists. A design call: fail closed for replay tasks.
9. **The binomial test is only informational** (J:1007-1046). `LEAKY` is decided by a permutation test, not the spec's binomial p&lt;0.05. This is a design call with a better statistical basis, but it deviates from the spec and the deviation should be recorded.
10. **Only above-chance accuracy is tested** (J:989-1004). A judge that consistently guesses the wrong arm still knows the identity and goes undetected. Real but minor. The "blind" fixture at T:298-305 derives its guesses from the true arms, so it passes by coincidence.
11. **`binom_sf` can overflow or underflow on large n** (J:965). Real but low severity. `math.comb(n,i) * float` raises `OverflowError` when n is above roughly 1000, and the reference binomial is computed for every row.</result>

## Claude

- `Agent — Fix round 2 for arena judge`

## Claude

The blind GPT review of the step 4 judge is back. I've started fix round 2 on an Opus builder. The review used medium effort and finished in time, and the helper checked the cited lines for findings 1, 2, 3, 7 and 11 only.

Findings I'm treating as real, each getting a forced-failure test:
- **`sys.exit(0)` counts as a pass.** A hidden check whose import raises `SystemExit(0)` is scored as a pass. Round 1 only covered `os._exit`. The fix requires the nonce marker for every pass.
- **Repo-configured `textconv`.** It can run a command during diff generation. The fix runs every git call with external diff, textconv and hooks disabled, under a scrubbed environment.
- **Filename handling.** `--name-only` without `-z` quotes odd filenames and silently drops their changes.
- **Missing hidden-check files fail open.** Replay tasks must fail closed.
- **Judges share one work directory** and an inherited environment. Each gets a private workspace.
- **Two-sided guess test.** The guess test only checks above-chance accuracy, so a judge that consistently guesses the wrong arm is missed. The "blind" fixture also derives its guesses from the true arms.
- **`binom_sf` overflow.** It fails for large n.
- **Glued names.** The neutraliser misses names like `xralph_afoo`.

The agent also settles two design calls:
- **Leaky GPT route:** the GPT judge gets only a copy of the neutralised diff inside the prompt, with no tree or workspace path. The Opus judge gets only the neutralised diff and no path to the raw tree.
- **Spec deviation:** the permutation test, not the spec's binomial p<0.05, decides LEAKY. That gets recorded in the docstring and the report, with the binomial p still printed.

The Windows `codex.exe` reach of `\\wsl.localhost` paths remains. Removing paths from the prompt means there is nothing there to read.

Four agents are running: fix rounds for steps 1, 2 and 4, and the GPT review of step 3. The metering tester is still queued.

I edited no code, made no commit and did not touch the ledger.

Ranked findings, with my triage:
1. **Critical, real in principle (design call on the mitigation).** The candidate's selftest runs in the same process as the runner (`arena_gate.py:258-315, 670-763`). It can monkeypatch `json.dumps` so a failing test serialises as a signed pass. Fixing it needs the record written by a separate process or an out-of-process check on the exit code and counts.
2. **Critical, real in principle.** Candidate tests can overwrite later instruments (validator, winbuild, deploy, proof, base selftest) because `tool_tainted()` only checks committed paths (`:442-453`). The fix is to make the instrument checkouts read-only or take them from the base.
3. **High, real.** `s_tests()` (`:725-755`) never checks `rc`. A test that hangs after writing a valid pass record times out with `rc=None` but still passes. That breaks "cannot run means UNMEASURED."
4. **High, real, design call.** `finish()` (`:1064-1094`) leaves `passed=True` and `verdict="pass"` when signing fails, and only adds `record_error`. I did not read the CLI exit code in `main()`. It should return invalid or a non-zero exit.
5. **High, partly real.** `s_live` and `s_test_record` (`:920-981`) filter records only by task and sha. The last canary wins, so an earlier failed canary is ignored, and old and new records can mix. Using the last canary may be intended ("re-queued"), but there is no lane, `run_id` or ordering match.
6. **High, real.** `s_csharp` and the proof step accept any exit 0 (`:795-817`). A proof that prints "UNMEASURED" and exits 0 passes, because `UNMEASURED_PHRASE` is only checked in tests.
7. **Medium, real.** Deleting a `.py`, `.xml` or `.cs` file plus adding an in-area data file leaves tests, XML and C# as NA.
8. **Medium, real but minor.** `def bar(): pass; return None` evades the stub check. `.js` and `.ts` are outside `CODE_EXT`. A native `.so` shadowing a stdlib module is not caught.
9. **Medium, minor.** `verdict_of` (`:1052-1062`) lets an unknown status such as `None` or `BROKEN` through. Nothing currently produces one, but the reducer should compare against `PASS` or `NA` only.

GPT also says the selftest does not prove these:
- runner isolation
- a valid record followed by a timeout
- replay or ordering of records
- the fact that signing failure still yields a pass
- the real `run_selftests.py --only` path (its stub at `selftest_arena_gate.py:46-52` is unused)</result>

## Claude

- `Agent — Fix round 2 for arena gate`

## Claude

The blind GPT review of the step 3 gate is back, and I've started fix round 2 on an Opus builder. GPT worked at medium effort without running code. The helper read only some of the cited lines of `arena_gate.py` (725–755, 795–817, 915–985 and 1050–1100), so the rest of the triage is from GPT's own descriptions.

**Two critical findings:**
- **Same-process forgery.** The candidate's selftest runs in the same process as the runner, so it can monkeypatch `json.dumps` and make a failing test serialise as a signed pass. The helper judged this real in principle. The verdict has to come from the parent process, built from the child's exit status and records the parent parses itself.
- **Instrument overwrite.** Candidate tests can overwrite later instruments (the validator, winbuild, deploy, proof and base selftest), because `tool_tainted()` checks only committed paths. Instruments need to come from the base checkout or a read-only mount.

**High:**
- **Hang after a valid pass.** `s_tests()` never checks the return code, so a test that hangs after writing a valid pass record times out but still passes.
- **Signing failure.** `finish()` leaves `passed=True` and `verdict="pass"` when signing fails. It should return invalid and a non-zero exit.
- **Canary and test records.** They are filtered only by task and sha, and the last canary wins, so an earlier failed canary is ignored.
- **Proof exit code.** A proof that prints UNMEASURED and exits 0 passes.

**Medium:**
- **Deleted code files.** Deleting a `.py`, `.xml` or `.cs` file plus adding an in-area data file leaves tests, XML and C# as NA.
- **Stub check.** `def bar(): pass; return None` evades it, and `.js` and `.ts` files aren't covered.
- **Unknown statuses.** The verdict reducer lets an unknown status such as `None` or `BROKEN` through.

GPT also says the selftest doesn't prove runner isolation, a valid record followed by a timeout, record replay or ordering, signing failure ending in a non-pass, or the real `run_selftests.py --only` path.

The fix round covers all of the above, and the round 1 leftovers: binary-only diffs, `*` matching across directories, and code hidden under bookkeeping paths. It has to state exactly what same-process forgery risk remains.

Four agents are running: fix rounds for steps 1, 2, 3 and 4. The metering tester is still queued.

<result>GPT's blind round 2 is saved to `D:\ClaudeWork\rimmandrake\foundry_arena\judge_gpt_review2.md`. It raised 12 findings, listed below with my triage. I did not run any code or edit anything.

1. **Windows `codex.exe` is not confined by the Linux sandbox** (`arena_judge.py:97-101`, criterion "judge cannot open the arm map"): residual limit. The module already states it in its RESIDUAL block. It gets only the neutralised prompt, and the selftest uses a Linux fake model.
2. **Same-user arms can read and relocate the map and edit hidden checks or verdicts** (`arena_judge.py:217-258`): residual limit. This is the same-OS-user premise, which no file permissions can fix.
3. **Arm-controlled code can recover the nonce from `_main()` locals and forge the completion marker** (`arena_judge.py:834-850`, `874-881`): real. `nonce` is a live local while `exec(code)` runs, so a replay's hidden result can read `pass`. The fix is to keep the nonce out of reachable frames, for example by checking the marker from the parent side.
4. **A replay with `{}` or an empty `checks` list passes the guard** (`arena_judge.py:1180-1183`): real. The guard rejects only `hidden is None`, so an empty or all-`UNMEASURED` check set reaches the models.
5. **A record with a list-valued `judge_id` or `judge` crashes `write_report()`** (`arena_judge.py:1332`, `1340`, `1375-1380`): real but low severity. It needs a malformed or arm-edited verdict file; the fix is an `isinstance(str)` check.
6. **The neutraliser's forced-failure fixture trips several detectors at once** (`selftest_arena_judge.py:95-99`, `145-150`): real. The test cannot show the arm-name detector alone fires.
7. **Masks cover only the configured global roots, not an arbitrary `clone` or `work_base`** (`arena_judge.py:312-325`, `1156-1167`): design call. Production paths sit under the configured roots.
8. **Opus uses the real home directory, and GPT uses shared scratch** (`arena_judge.py:1006-1069`, `204-209`): design call plus residual limit. This is the same-user premise again, and `opus_model()` is not locked to Opus.
9. **The `trio` and `v3.2` exemptions leave literal identity strings in the diff** (`arena_judge.py:440-456`, `481-482`): design call. They are needed to keep code legible, and the guess-rate test measures what leaks.
10. **A "Binary files differ" summary counts as a nonempty diff** (`arena_judge.py:723-727`): real, minor. `DIFF_SAFE` (line 632) has no `--binary`, so a binary change reaches the judges as an opaque one-line summary. Rejecting binary diffs, or flagging them, would close it.
11. **The permutation test uses overall accuracy only** (`arena_judge.py:1295-1298`): design call. A deterministic mapping that guesses at chance accuracy but reveals the arm would look clean. A stricter statistic such as mutual information would catch it.
12. **`parse_answer` takes the last decodable object containing `"verdict"`** (`arena_judge.py:972-990`): design call plus residual limit. A quoted example after a rejection could flip the result, and the diff sits inside the prompt, so injection is possible.

Items 3 and 4 are the ones that can falsely pass the "known-bad replay output is rejected" criterion. Items 1 and 2 are the stated map-access limit rather than new bugs.</result>

## Claude

The last blind GPT round on the step 4 judge (`ARENA_JUDGES_BUILD_BLIND_TO_THE_ARM`) is back with 12 findings. I started a final Opus fix round for the real ones. The review is at `D:\ClaudeWork\rimmandrake\foundry_arena\judge_gpt_review2.md`. The triage below is the helper's, from reading the cited lines; it ran no code.

**Real, and being fixed with a forced-failure test each:**
- **Nonce recovery (the serious one).** Arm-controlled code can recover the nonce from `_main()` locals while it runs and forge the completion marker, so a known-bad replay could read as a pass. The fix keeps the nonce out of anything the arm code can reach. The parent will observe completion over a channel the arm code cannot forge.
- **Empty hidden checks.** A replay with `{}` or an empty or all-UNMEASURED `checks` list passes the guard, so it reaches the models. The guard now fails closed unless at least one concrete check ran and passed.
- **Crash on a malformed verdict.** A list-valued `judge_id` crashes `write_report()`. It needs a malformed or arm-edited verdict file, so it's low severity.
- **Neutraliser fixture.** It trips several detectors at once, so the test can't show the arm-name detector alone fires. Each detector gets its own fixture.
- **Binary diffs.** A "Binary files differ" summary counts as a nonempty diff. The judges can't judge binary changes, so those are rejected.
- **Verdict parsing.** `parse_answer` takes the last decodable object containing `"verdict"`, so a quoted example after the answer can flip the result. Exactly one verdict object is now required.

**Design calls:**
- The permutation statistic uses overall accuracy only. A deterministic mapping at chance accuracy would look clean. The fixer will add a second statistic if it takes about 40 lines or fewer, and otherwise document it.
- The masks cover only the configured roots, not an arbitrary clone path.
- Opus uses the real home directory.
- The `trio` and `v3.2` exemptions leave identity strings in the diff.

**Residual limits, stated again:**
- **Windows route:** `codex.exe` can still reach the arm map over `\\wsl.localhost`.
- **Same-user premise:** a same-user arm can read and relocate the map, and edit hidden checks and verdicts.

8. **Stubs and scope:** `pass; return None` is caught, .js/.ts and other code types are scanned, and native .so/.pyd files fail scope.
9. **verdict_of:** only PASS and NA can pass.

## Claude

The last blind GPT round on the step 3 gate (`ARENA_GATE_BUILD_UNMEASURED_NEVER_PASSES`) found five issues. I've started a final Opus fix round for the ones the gate can fix. GPT's review is at `D:\ClaudeWork\rimmandrake\foundry_arena\gate_gpt_review2.md`. The helper read only part of the cited lines (560–595, 738–770 and 800–815 of `arena_gate.py`), so findings 2 and 3 are unverified.

**Residual limits (the same-user assumption):**
- **WSL interop bypass.** The sandbox keeps `WSL_INTEROP`, so candidate code could launch `wsl.exe` outside the sandbox and forge the different-agent and canary records.
- **Forged test result.** The candidate shares an interpreter with the runner, so it could write its own pass record. The tamper regex only blocks particular spellings.

The fix round will do best-effort on the first: scrub `WSL_INTEROP`, `WSL_DISTRO_NAME` and `WSLENV`, and mask `/run/WSL`. It will state what remains. A real fix needs a different OS identity or an isolated signing service.

**Fixing:**
- **Early exit.** Candidate code that calls `sys.exit(0)` could end an unchanged selftest early and be accepted. Passing will require proof that the checks completed, comparing the executed-check count with a count taken before the candidate runs.
- **Coverage.** One covered change can hide an uncovered one. Line 764 flags an unmapped file only when it was deleted, so a modified unmapped file next to a mapped one leaves nothing flagged. Every changed code file will need coverage, or the tests step reads UNMEASURED. Helper confirmed this one by reading.
- **Writable build.** The head checkout is writable during the winbuild step, so a build target could rewrite files earlier steps already passed. The build will move to a throwaway copy.

The round also folds in the two integration problems I filed:
- **Event log selftest:** it refuses any entry script but its own, so event log tasks always fail under the gate.
- **Live records:** the real `arena_lane` records use verdict ok/error with no sha.

The gate side will be made ready. If `arena_eventlog.py` needs a change, the fixer will describe it exactly instead of editing it.

Four agents are running: the step 1 fix round, the final judge and gate fixes, and the metering GPT review.

Findings, ranked by severity, with my triage:
1. **Slug collisions** (`spend_census.py:242-244`, `273-281`). Design call. `path_slug` maps every non-alphanumeric to `-`, so `/arena/solo` and `/arena-solo` encode the same. Ambiguity is only resolved against the real tree when more than one name matches, and a deleted clone's slug can bill to a sibling. Claude Code's own encoding causes this. The fix is to refuse arm names whose slugs collide, or to cross-check the cwd stored in the transcript.
2. **GPT `tokens_used` added 1:1 to Claude ITE** (`spend_census.py:358-364`, `411`). Real, but already documented as a convention in the docstring. It is the unit-mixing risk, so ask the owner or the spec whether to report GPT separately. The per-arm GPT and Claude fields already exist.
3. **Selftest never launches a fresh-clone probe** (`selftest_arena_meter.py:221-229`). Real against the literal criterion. It copies a transcript fixture and builds the slug with the code's own `path_slug`. The live probe is still owed, and the check skips silently if the fixture is missing.
4. **No-ID fallback hash collapses identical calls** (`spend_census.py:99-103`). Not real in practice, because real transcripts carry message IDs. It is an edge case only.
5. **First-record-wins dedup with mtime ownership** (`spend_census.py:102-104`, `333-341`). Design call. The selftest blesses it explicitly, and a conflict flag is raised.
6. **Zero-spend escape routes** (`spend_census.py:391-395`, `412-413`). Partly real. The `idle_arms` path is intended and flagged. `since=0.0` as the default window lets old spend satisfy an arm. The CLI should require a window.
7. **`RM_GPT_CALL_LOG` file override skips the drvfs safety check** (`gpt_call_log.py:70-74`, `95-98`). Real but minor. An explicit override is the caller's choice. A short-write check is cheap to add.
8. **GPT records with no numeric `t` bypass the window** (`spend_census.py:380-382`). Real. Such records should be excluded or counted as unreadable, not included.
9. **Bad transcript line or timestamp can drop a file's spend while its IDs stay in `seen`** (`spend_census.py:67-70`, `104-105`). Plausible. I did not read these lines.
10. **A failed GPT log write leaves no record** (`gpt_consult.py:68-73`). Design call. The consult call is deliberately non-fatal, and it prints a warning.
11. **Hardcoded paths** (`gpt_call_log.py:21-22`, `spend_census.py:28`). The `/mnt/d` fallback is the project convention. The Lodestar path is a personal-path smell, but it names no account, host or key.</result>

## Claude

The blind GPT review of step 6 metering (`ARENA_METER_BUILD_SPEND_BY_CLONE_PATH`) is back with 11 findings. The review is at `D:\ClaudeWork\rimmandrake\foundry_arena\meter_gpt_review.md`, and the helper checked only findings 1, 2, 6 and 8 against the code. I started a final Sonnet fix round for the real ones.

**Real, being fixed with a forced-failure test each:**
- **Default window of 0.0:** `since=0.0` lets old spend satisfy an arm. The CLI will require an explicit `--since` when `--ran` is used.
- **GPT records with no numeric `t`:** they bypass the window. They'll be excluded and counted as unreadable.
- **Malformed transcript line or timestamp:** it can drop a file's spend while its message ids stay marked as seen. An id will be marked seen only after its usage is counted.
- **`RM_GPT_CALL_LOG` override:** it skips the drvfs safety check. It will read back the line it wrote and warn.
- **Slug collisions:** `/arena/solo` and `/arena-solo` encode the same. Arm names whose slugs collide will be refused at declaration, and a transcript's own recorded `cwd` will be cross-checked against the arm path.
- **Fresh-clone probe not launched:** the selftest never launches the fresh-clone probe the criterion asks for. It builds the slug with the code's own function and skips silently if the fixture is missing. It will now print a loud `OWED:` line and fail if the fixture is missing.

**Needs your decision.** GPT `tokens_used` is added 1:1 to Claude ITE, which mixes units. The fix reports Claude ITE and GPT tokens as separate per-arm fields. Any combined total carries an explicit note and no invented conversion. The open question is whether GPT tokens should be converted to ITE for the headline measure.

**Not changing:** the no-id fallback hash (an edge case, since real transcripts carry ids), earliest-transcript ownership (the selftest blesses it), a failed GPT log write being non-fatal (deliberate), and the hardcoded paths (they follow project convention).

The real fresh-arm-clone job stays owed. Four agents are running: the step 1 fix round, and the final fixes for the judge, gate and metering.

## Claude

I claimed step 5, `ARENA_REPLAY_BUILD_NO_FUTURE_IN_CLONE`, and started a Sonnet agent building it. It's Sonnet work because tests can check it. Nothing is built yet.

The agent builds `arena_replay.py`, which creates each replay clone with `git init` plus a fetch of only the base sha. It schedules runs interleaved and randomised per arm from a seed derived from the task freeze hash. It adds the replay tree to the `rm-scratch-reap` roots and deletes each clone after judging, including on failure. Clones are full clones, never worktrees.

The selftest must prove three things by forced failure:
- a replay clone cannot `git show` the original fix sha, including via packed objects, alternates, the reflog, `FETCH_HEAD`, remote refs, or `cat-file --batch-all-objects`;
- the run order differs per arm and is reproducible from the seed;
- a judged clone is gone, also after an exception mid-run.

The agent reuses the sibling arena modules read-only and can't edit them.

Four agents are running: the replay build, the step 1 fix round, and the final judge and gate fixes. The independent test and blind GPT review for replay come after the build lands.

## Claude

- `Agent — Independent test of arena_replay`

## Claude

Step 5, the replay runner (`ARENA_REPLAY_BUILD_NO_FUTURE_IN_CLONE`), is built and published as `72297ab3d3`. It's uncertified: the code is DIRTY, and an independent Opus tester is now running against it.

The selftest passes. It catches a naive full clone, a `--shared` clone and a fully packed clone, and a fix sha planted in `FETCH_HEAD`, packed-refs, a reflog or a ref file. A base sha that already contains the fix is refused and leaves no directory behind. Run order is the same for the same hash, differs for a different hash, and differs across all four arms. A clone left behind after judging is detected, including after an exception.

How it excludes future objects:
- **Clone build:** `git init --template=` and a fetch of only the base sha. There is no remote, no tags, no `FETCH_HEAD` and no reflog, and `HEAD` is set to the base sha.
- **Audit:** an audit fails the build if the fix sha appears under `cat-file -e`, `git show` or `--batch-all-objects`. It also fails on alternates, a commondir file, any ref naming the sha, or a remote or branch in the config.

Choices to be aware of:
- **Own clone builder:** `arena_tasks.build_arm_clone` is depth-1 and made for arm clones, so the replay runner has its own builder to the §1.4 spec.
- **Arena root:** it comes from `RM_ARENA_ROOT` or a `root=` argument, and the runner refuses anything under `/mnt/`.
- **Seeds:** each arm's seed is `sha256(freeze_hash|arm)`. A repeated draw redraws from the same stream.
- **Audit scope:** the audit forbids the fix sha plus any `also_forbidden` shas the caller passes, not every hidden blob.
- **Reaper root:** a one-line edit adds `/home/mandrake/rm/arena/replay` to `DEFAULT_ROOTS` in `scratch_reap.py`. Its selftest still passes.

The tester probes every route to the fix sha, a clone that can re-fetch later, seeding edge cases and hash randomisation, cleanup after a kill, and path-traversal names. A blind GPT review follows.

Four agents are running: the replay tester, the step 1 fix round, and the final judge and gate fixes.

- **Medium, D3:** the cleanup's error handler crashes with a TypeError under Python 3.14 when the judge leaves a chmod-000 directory. The clone survives and the verdict is lost.

  - L2: the freeze hash is not validated or normalised; uppercase or whitespace silently gives a different schedule.
  - L3: a root of exactly `/mnt` is accepted, and a relative `RM_ARENA_ROOT` stays relative.
  - L4: the audit uses the caller's environment, so `GIT_DIR` gives a false leak.
  - L5: a symlinked arm directory is followed.

## Claude

The independent test of the step 5 replay runner (`ARENA_REPLAY_BUILD_NO_FUTURE_IN_CLONE`) found one high-severity defect, and the last GPT round on step 1 (`ARENA_TASKS_BUILD_FROZEN_BEFORE_ASSIGNED`) is back. I've started a fix agent for each. Nothing is filed in the ledger from either report.

**Replay runner: two criteria pass, one high defect.**
- **Fix sha is unreachable.** The fix, annotated tag, notes and merge commit stay unreachable by every route, including after `gc --prune=now`. A base that reaches the fix through a merge is refused. The 6 probe failures were the tester's own mistakes or env-var cases.
- **Order.** It is identical across hash seeds, locales and time zones, and distinct per arm.
- **High defect: it can delete a clone it never created.** When `run_one` refuses because the clone path already exists, its cleanup still deletes that path. The tester reproduced three cases:
  - two concurrent runs of the same (arm, task), where the second run's refusal deleted the first run's live clone mid-work;
  - a symlinked arm directory, where data outside the arena root was deleted;
  - a pre-existing directory at the clone path.
- **Medium defects:**
  - **Reaper edit has no effect.** `scratch_reap.py` refuses every replay clone ("no remote at all"), so clones left by SIGKILL are never reaped. Only `arena_replay.py sweep` removes them.
  - **Python 3.14 crash.** The cleanup error handler hits a TypeError when the judge leaves a chmod-000 directory, so the verdict is lost.
  - **Design gap.** An arm can still `git fetch` the fix from the source repo by path, because it sits on the same filesystem.
- **Low:** a trailing newline passes name and sha validation. The freeze hash isn't normalised. A root of exactly `/mnt` is accepted. The audit uses the caller's environment, so `GIT_DIR` gives a false leak. A symlinked arm directory is followed.

The fix round deletes only a clone the run created itself, with exclusive creation and path checks. It also makes the reaper accept a marked arena replay clone after a time limit, fixes the Python 3.14 handler, and scrubs the git environment. The fetch source moves to a private harness mirror. It states the same-user fetch gap plainly and adds a function listing the paths a sandbox must hide.

**Step 1 GPT round (the last allowed): three criterion-breaking items, helper triage is a guess.** The helper opened none of the cited lines.
- **Residual limits:** a same-user arm can open the judge's hidden checks directly, and could rewrite the freeze hash, batch manifest and chain and mirror the event log. These only matter if the criterion means a sandbox boundary. They go into the module as a plain note.
- **Real, cheap to fix:** there is no gzip decoding, so after assignment a hidden test placed in `notes.gz` evades the scan.
- **Design call:** prose is read at the closing commit's parent, not at claim time. The final fix tags each task with its prompt source and flags prompts that may hold hints. It adds no behaviour change.

Four agents are running: the replay fix, the step 1 final fix, and the final judge and gate fixes.

Its selftest passes 157 checks, the 134 old plus 23 new, and the UNMEASURED-as-pass mutation stays red with 31 failures. The code is DIRTY. One cross-file change remains in the event log, and I started a Sonnet agent on it.

**Fixed, each with a test:**
- **Early exit:** a collection pass before any candidate code counts the selftest's test functions. The result line carries done and expected counts, and done below expected reads UNMEASURED. `sys.exit(0)` from inside a test, or at import, now reads UNMEASURED.
- **Uncovered files:** every changed `.py`, `.cs` and `.xml` file needs its own selftest or step. A modified `foo_extra.py` next to a covered change reads UNMEASURED and names the file.
- **Writable build:** the build runs in a throwaway copy, and head, base and the runner directory are fingerprinted before and after. A build that rewrites any of them is a csharp FAIL.
- **WSL interop:** `WSL_*` variables are removed, `/run/WSL` is emptied and `/init` is covered, for every step except the build. On this host `cmd.exe /c echo` fails with "Permission denied" in every step except the build's sandbox.
- **Live step:** it accepts the real `arena_lane` records, matched by run id, and checks the shape rather than a `writer` field the signed log rejects. A live_release with verdict `ok` passes. A verdict of `error` or an aborted hold reads UNMEASURED, and a canary with problems reads INVALID.

**Residual risk:**
- **Same interpreter:** candidate code shares the runner's interpreter and can still forge the result line and the new counts.
- **WSL interop:** it is closed best-effort only, because `binfmt_misc` is kernel-wide and the `/init` socket is undocumented.
- **MSBuild:** it runs on the Windows side outside the sandbox, so a build that rewrites the checkout is detected, not prevented.

**The event log change.** `arena_eventlog.py` has to accept the gate's runner as an entry script. Until it does, tasks touching the event log always FAIL under the gate. The gate agent wasn't allowed to edit that file, so it described the exact change and I handed it to the Sonnet agent, which also adds tests.

Four agents are running: the replay fix, the step 1 final fix, the event log entry change, and the final judge fix.

## Claude

The event log now accepts the gate runner as an entry script, so tasks touching `arena_eventlog` should no longer always fail under the gate. The change is published as `85ecec3e5a`. The code is DIRTY and has not been through code review.

- **Change:** `_check_entrypoint` first asks `arena_gate.gate_runner_selftest` whether this process is the gate runner. It reads the command line from `/proc/self/cmdline` and imports `arena_gate` lazily. If so, the selftest must sit in the same directory, its name must start with `selftest_arena_`, and the runner file is code-checked in place of the entry file. When the gate hook says no, every existing refusal is unchanged.
- **Accepted:** the real runner launched with the proper command line and environment marker, recording the entry as `selftest_arena_eventlog.py`.
- **Refused:** no marker, a wrong sha, a runner copy (even with the marker set to the copy's own sha), a spoofed `__main__.__file__` with the marker set, and bad selftest paths (outside the prefix, not a selftest, wrong directory).
- **Weaker check:** a selftest copied elsewhere is only tested for "no pass opens". The copy dies on import before it can refuse.
- **Results:** 9 new checks were added, and the existing checks stay green. The event log and gate selftests both pass.

Four agents are running: the replay GPT review, the switch build (step 7), and the final judge fix.

## Claude

Three things happened since the last report.

**My Bash tool wedged and is recovered.** The first step 7 switch helper started a private `systemd --user` to try a throwaway session. That enabled the memory controller on this seat's `claude-code-bash` cgroup while processes lived there, so every Bash call failed with `EBUSY` on `clone3`. I cleared it by writing `-memory` to that cgroup's `subtree_control` with the Write tool, and Bash works again. The helper's scratch directory `/home/mandrake/.seat-tmp/FOUNDRY/probe` is still there. It contains root-owned `run/systemd/inaccessible` entries that `rm` can't remove. I filed a lesson (`20261030T034001Z-FOUNDRY-never-start-private-systemd-user-or.md`, uncommitted) saying never to start a private manager from a seat.

**Step 4 judge is done.** The final fix is published as `f1b44647f9` and the ledger as `645274a4b`. The helper's Bash broke before it could commit, so I ran the selftest and published its work myself. It passes, and the code is DIRTY. One check was never shown: the new tests failing against the previous commit. The helper's fixes:
- the nonce is sealed away from arm code, with an audit hook blocking the ways to reach it;
- a replay whose hidden file runs nothing is invalid before any model call;
- a binary diff is a refusal;
- exactly one verdict object is required;
- the guess test gets a second statistic (mutual information), reported per judge and pooled;
- each neutraliser detector has its own fixture.

The same-user and Windows `codex.exe` limits are in the docstring.

**Step 5 replay: the blind GPT review found real defects, and fix round 2 is running.**
- **Sweep and symlinks:** `sweep` follows a symlink at `replay` and deletes without checking the marker or the run lock. A sweep during a live run deletes the active clone.
- **Marker handling:** a marker written as a JSON list raises an uncaught `AttributeError`, so the judged clone is left behind.
- **SIGKILL during build:** the marker is written only after the fetch and audit, so such a clone is never reaped.
- **Git environment:** `work()` and `judge()` get the raw environment. An inherited `GIT_DIR` could let the arm see the fix while the audit passes.
- **Audit:** it treats any nonzero git exit as "absent" and ignores `core.hooksPath` and filters.
- **Schedule:** one arm's run order depends on the other arms.
- **Lock name:** lock names can collide.

**Step 7 switch** is restarted on a Sonnet agent with the systemd rule spelled out. It rehearses only with a fake `systemctl` shim. The event log's `ENTRYPOINTS` doesn't allow a new `arena.py` to sign yet, so that is listed as owed.

I also started step 8, `ARENA_RALPHA_BUILD_TESTER_PASS_EACH_BUILD`, on an Opus builder. It builds `ralph_a` with a blind tester pass after every build. The loop must refuse a second build pass with no `test_start` from the tester for the first. Its model calls are injectable so the selftest costs nothing.

Three agents are running: the switch build, the replay fix and the `ralph_a` build.

- **#9:** Each arm's order is derived only from sha256(freeze|arm). On a collision the later arm (by sorted name) gets a deterministic salt increment, and `schedule` raises if a duplicate is still left. Tests: one arm's order is the same whatever the other arms are, a collision forced with a fake hash is resolved reproducibly, and an unresolvable collision raises.
- **#10:** `_lock` validates names first and names the lock file by sha256 of the NUL-joined names. Tests: a bad name is refused before any lock directory exists, and `a__b`+`c` and `a`+`b__c` no longer collide.
- **Not mine (#1, #5, #8):** A RESIDUAL paragraph is added to the module docstring.

## Claude

Step 5, the replay runner (`ARENA_REPLAY_BUILD_NO_FUTURE_IN_CLONE`), is recorded as done at `5489d80a3d`. The ledger is published as `f0647ce05`. The replay and `scratch_reap` selftests both pass, but the fixer did not run a red-before pass, so the new tests were not shown to fail on the old code.

**Fixed in round 2:**
- **Sweep:** `sweep` refuses a symlinked replay or arm directory, and any clone without a valid marker. It skips a clone whose run lock is held, so a sweep during a live run leaves the clone alone.
- **Cleanup:** it trusts only the inode and nonce the run recorded, not the on-disk marker. A list, garbage or wrong-nonce marker no longer crashes it or leaves a judged clone.
- **Killed builds:** the marker is written atomically right after `mkdir` with state `building`. A run SIGKILLed mid-build now leaves a marked clone that the reaper takes after the time limit.
- **Git environment:** `work()` and `judge()` run with all `GIT_*` variables dropped and a private HOME. An inherited `GIT_DIR` is not visible to either.
- **Audit:** git exit 1 means absent and any other exit fails the audit. It refuses a clone config with `hooksPath`, fsmonitor, filter, textconv or includeIf, and any active hook.
- **Schedule:** each arm's run order depends only on its own hash. Collisions get a deterministic salt increment, and the schedule raises if one remains.
- **Lock names:** lock names are validated first and hashed, so `a__b`+`c` and `a`+`b__c` no longer collide.

The same-user limits are in the module docstring.

I also committed two lessons with that ledger entry: the private-`systemd --user` wedge, and the leak scan trusting an arm-writable marker. The code is DIRTY.

Steps 1 through 6 are now closed or partly closed:
- Steps 1, 2, 3, 4 and 5 are done.
- Step 6 (metering) has its live probe owed.
- Steps 7 (the switch) and 8 (`ralph_a`) are being built, with two agents running.

## Claude

Step 7, the arena switch (`ARENA_SWITCH_BUILD_ONE_ARM_LIVE_ONLY`), is built and published as `121a67279a` (published sha `d32f81666d`). It is uncertified: the code is DIRTY, and an independent Opus tester is now running. The new selftest has 101 checks and passes, and `selftest_ops_watch` passes too.

**What was proven in the throwaway world** (a temp directory with a fake `systemctl`, real git clones with bare remotes, and a fake clock):
- **Two targets:** starting two arm targets fails for all 15 pairs, production included, and leaves state unchanged. Units rendered without `Conflicts=` do start together, so the shim can tell the difference.
- **Stopped-arm pushes:** a push from a stopped arm is refused and alarmed in both sinks. A live arm pushing main, another arm's namespace, or a deletion is also refused, and so is an unreadable `arm.toml`.
- **`arena who`:** it exits 1, 2, 4 and 8 for its four checks, and the codes combine. A reader that raises fails its check.
- **Switch refusals:** each of these leaves the new target unstarted:
  - never idle, forced stop at 90 minutes on the fake clock;
  - an unpushed commit, with the old target left live;
  - signer failure;
  - bridge release failure;
  - a restore that leaves different `activeMods`.
- **Production rehearsal:** switching `ralph_a` to `production` produced a signed event that `arena_eventlog.validate_record` accepts, and `arena who` returned 0 afterwards. No real systemd, dbus, cgroup or user unit was touched.

**Choices to be aware of:**
- **Unpushed check order:** it runs before the old target is stopped, which deviates from design 2.2. The STOP file stays on a refusal.
- **Signer failure:** it is fatal.
- **Trial mode:** `trial` expects `rm-arm-v3.target`, and `v3` and `production` share the bench and foundry clones.
- **Bridge holder:** the holder's arm is parsed from the purpose string `arena <arm> ...`, because rimflow has one shared lock.

**Owed:**
- **Event log entry:** `arena.py` has to be added to `arena_eventlog.ENTRYPOINTS` before it can sign in production. I started a Sonnet agent for that. It also checks that `switch` events validate.
- **Real `arm.toml`:** it has to be created outside git, with each arm's real unit lists.
- **Installer `--apply`:** it needs a `daemon-reload` and is an owner decision.

The tester has a hard safety rule: no systemd, dbus, cgroup or real unit access. Three agents are running: the switch tester, the entry change, and the `ralph_a` build.

## Claude

The independent test of step 7, the arena switch (`ARENA_SWITCH_BUILD_ONE_ARM_LIVE_ONLY`), found real defects. It does not meet its criteria as built, so I started an Opus fix round. The tester changed no code and filed nothing.

**Criteria results:**
- **Two targets fail to start:** this holds only at the target level. The tester's own systemd model confirmed that `Conflicts=` is declared both ways for all 30 arm pairs, and that starting B after A stops A.
- **Stopped-arm push refused and alarmed:** this fails as built.
- **`arena who` exit codes:** each check fails alone with exit 1, 2, 4 or 8, and all four give 15. Other `who` defects are below.
- **Production switch rehearsal:** fails as a criterion. It was done only with the tester's fake `systemctl`, not a throwaway user session, which the safety rule forbids. It is still owed.

**High defects:**
- **Hook never runs on bench and foundry.** Those clones set `core.hooksPath`, so git never runs the installed pre-push hook. A stopped production clone pushes main without an alarm.
- **Nothing reads the push alarm file.**
- **Trial mode.** It starts only the v3 target, so `who` checks (c) and (d) fail for the whole trial. There is no unit for the trial's replay runner.
- **Enabled timers.** Only targets are guarded. The v3 timers stay enabled, so after a reboot four arms' units run while `who` check (a) stays green.

**Medium defects:**
- **Unpushed check:** it misses detached HEAD, stash, untracked or dirty files, submodules, other remotes and a missing clone. A switch out of production with a dirty foundry went through.
- **No switch lock.**
- **Already-live mode:** switching to the live mode restarts it after up to 90 minutes.
- **Interrupted switch:** it leaves the live arm paused while `who` reads 0.
- **Hook scope:** the hook ignores `[production] scope`.

**`who` problems:** trial mode alarms on itself, and untagged locks pass. Only one shared bridge lock is read. A corrupt `arm.toml` exits 1, the same as check (a). A stale STOP file and a stale idle status go unseen.

**Low:** a hook crash writes no alarm, and a `$(...)` in the `arm.toml` path runs as a command.

**Limits that stay:**
- **Client-side hook:** force pushes, `--no-verify`, a repointed `arm.toml` and production deletes all get through it. Only per-arm credentials plus GitHub rulesets make it unbypassable, and that is an owner decision. The fix round adds a `server_side_requirements` note.
- **Rehearsal:** the production rehearsal can only use the shim.

Two agents are running: the switch fix and the `ralph_a` tester.

## Claude

The independent test of step 8 (`ARENA_RALPHA_BUILD_TESTER_PASS_EACH_BUILD`) found one high defect and six medium ones. I started an Opus fix round, and the report has not been filed in the ledger.

**What passed:**
- **§8 scenarios:** all 18 pass. A planted gate failure and a killed pass both recover, and a planted defect that passes the weak gate is caught by the tester.
- **Refusing a second build with no `test_start`:** this passes against id variants (case, whitespace, zero-width, fullwidth), the wrong task, build or arm, replays, clock skew, and unsigned, forged or truncated lines.
- **`bridge release --mine`:** it passes apart from one race, and rimflow's own selftests pass 5/5.
- **Runway check:** it fails closed on unmeasured, NaN or garbled readings. The changed code names no host, account or model id.

**High defect.** `--quick` silently drops a slow test whose source changed or that is currently red, and does not list it as SKIPPED. It reported GREEN where the full run is RED (2 of 4 failing), and the loop uses it as its pre-check. The fix only lets it skip tests that last passed with unchanged source, prints each as SKIPPED(quick), and labels the result as quick so it can't pass for a full GREEN.

**Medium defects:**
- **Crash recovery:** a crash between `build_end` and saving state wedges the loop on NotTested forever.
- **Orphaned model:** it outlives a killed loop and keeps writing in the clone while the restarted loop builds and publishes.
- **Tester isolation:** the tester can read the builder's transcripts and `last_failure.md` via `../../../`. Its environment carries `RALPH_A_ROOT`, `RM_ARM` and the arm name in its agent id.
- **Release race:** a take landing between `--mine`'s check and the release gets released out from under another seat.
- **Event log:** with `state.json` lost, a deleted, truncated or corrupted log lets a build run, because only the signature chain is checked, not the anchors.

**Low:** homoglyph ids count as a different agent, a duplicate `build_end` wedges an in-memory log, `TESTER.md` names the arm, and runway isn't rechecked before the tester pass. A negative runway threshold is accepted, and the git pull step is missing.

The fix round also owes one handover: `arena_eventlog.py` has to get `ralph_a_loop.py` added to `ENTRYPOINTS`. The agent will describe that exact change and not make it.

Two agents are running: the switch fix and the `ralph_a` fix.

## Claude

## Claude

The blind GPT review of the step 7 switch (`ARENA_SWITCH_BUILD_ONE_ARM_LIVE_ONLY`) is back. I started an Opus fix round 2 to read the cited code and fix what is real. The reviewer opened only the code for items 3 and 7, so the other triage notes are unchecked. GPT's line numbers are approximate, and the review is at `D:\ClaudeWork\rimmandrake\foundry_arena\switch_gpt_review.md`.

**Real, being fixed:**
- **Production unit set.** The production target has `units = []`, so "production live" checks nothing. The fix reads the real seat unit files (by file listing only, no `systemctl`) and puts the production set in the template. Units whose role it can't tell go under "unclassified" in the report.
- **Stale unpushed check.** It uses cached `--remotes=origin` with no fetch, so it can pass on a stale tracking ref after a remote force-replace or delete. Local-only tags are not covered. The fix refreshes first and fails closed if the remote is unreachable.
- **Fail-open evidence.** `who(d)` relies on commit timestamps and fails open on unreadable evidence. A failed `is-active` or `is-enabled` call counts as false and will be flagged as unknown.
- **Retry after partial failure.** It returns `already_live` without a full `who`.
- **Selftest gaps (the most important finding):**
  - The production rehearsal uses a fake manager in a temp directory.
  - The production-mode test compares XML that already matches, so it never exercises restore.
  - The CLI non-zero exit is tested for check a only, and checks b to d are tested by returned dictionaries.
  - The fix adds a differing ModsConfig that proves restore ran, subprocess exit tests for checks b to d, and ports the tester's more faithful systemd cases.

**To check in the code:**
- **Bridges and ModsConfig:** whether the switch releases both bridges and restores ModsConfig regardless of scope. The fix makes both scope-aware, and production scope must not clobber the owner's mod list.
- **Push versus switch:** hooks ignore the STOP file and switching state, and the dirt check runs before the writers stop. The fix makes the hook refuse pushes during a switch and rechecks the dirt under the lock.
- **Direct member start:** `PartOf=` does not block a direct start of a member unit. The fix adds `who` detection and a `ConditionPathExists=` gate on each member unit.

**Residual:** a same-user agent can bypass the hooks with `--no-verify`, edit them, or start units directly. The hook header already says it is client-side only.

Two agents are running: the switch fix and the `ralph_a` fix.

Both required selftests pass (arena_switch and ops_watch). As a red-before check, I reverted each main fix in turn with a scratch monkeypatch script, and every one turned its test red.

- **(3)** Production's live set comes from the real unit files and is written into `arm.toml.template` under `[arms.production.live]`. `who` (a) now fails when a listed unit is missing, no seat scope matches, or a unit's state is unknown, and the config load refuses an empty live list. Tested in `t_unknown_and_live`.
- **(4)** A switch releases only the stopping arm's bridge hold. It restores ModsConfig only when an arena arm stops, so leaving production never touches the owner's mod list. The production restore test checks the result by parsing ModsConfig.
- **(5)** The pre-push hook refuses while a switch is running, i.e. while the switch lock is held or a STOP file exists (production clones: STOP.v3 or STOP.production). Dirt that appears after the first check fails the switch before the new target starts. Tested in `t_prepush_and_late_dirt`.
- **(6)** "Already live" is only returned when a full `who` is green; otherwise the switch resumes. Tested in `t_already_live_resume`.
- **(7)** The unpushed check now catches local-only and moved tags, a remote force-replace, and a deleted remote branch. An unreachable origin refuses the switch, and `--allow-offline` lets it through and records that in the event. Tested in `t_remote_truth`.
- **(8)** `who` (d) fails on a corrupt state file, an unreadable events file or a git failure. Pushes are detected from a refs snapshot plus the reflog, so a commit with a 2001 date pushed after the switch is still caught. Tested in `t_who_d_fail_closed`.
- **(9)** A bus error is no longer read as inactive: `who` fails and the target is not treated as idle.
- **(2)** The member drop-in now requires a gate marker (ConditionPathExists=), with the PartOf limitation documented; tested on the unit text and in the fake systemctl.
- **(10)** The real CLI sets exit bits 2, 4 and 8 for checks b, c and d (`t_cli_bits`), and the fake systemctl agrees with the tester's fsd.py on all compared cases (`t_fsd_parity`).

**Unclassified production units** (neither production-live nor background): testq-sweep.timer, rm-scratch-reap.timer, rm-objstore.service, rm-v3-vs-v2, account-plan, claude-account-warm, hestia.
